Title: Collectio selecta SS. Ecclesiae Patrum, [tomus] LIII: Patres quarti Ecclesiae saeculi: S. Caesarius medicus, S. Didymus Caecus Alexandrinus, S. Phoebadius Aginnensis episcopus (S. Caesarii Dialogi IV; Didymi Caeci De Spiritu Sancto, Contra Manichaeos; S. Phoebadii Contra Arianos, De fide orthodoxa, De fide)
Creator: Phoebadius of Agen (fl. 357-392), with the works printed under the names of Caesarius (the Dialogues, now held to be a later pseudonymous work) and Didymus the Blind; edited in the Collectio selecta of A. B. Caillau and M. N. S. Guillon
Publisher: Parisiis: apud Parent-Desbarres (printed by Bourgogne et Martinet, file line 59); also sold at Montpellier
Publication Date: 1836 (archive.org catalogue date; the year is not legible in the OCR of the title page)
Rights: Public Domain (pd-us-by-date: an 1830s Paris printing); the item record carries no rights field
Language: lat (Latin only recovered; the Greek of the Caesarius and Didymus sections, where the edition prints any, was not recovered as Greek script)
Source: archive.org item scaesariidialogi00caes (University of Toronto copy), full text at https://archive.org/download/scaesariidialogi00caes/scaesariidialogi00caes_djvu.txt. Fetched directly 2026-10-17 by the Shared-Events source-research thread (Era 2 worker A); the byte count of the downloaded file (897,416) equals the size archive.org lists for the djvu.txt.
Provenance note: Extracted unaltered: the item's own djvu.txt is reproduced whole after this header; nothing was stripped, normalized or corrected. Rights basis: an 1836 Paris printing is out of US copyright under this project's pd-us-by-date rule.
Content note: Vendored for the Phoebadius section (from file line 15473; the Liber contra Arianos from file line 15614; the Libellus fidei at file line 17790). Phoebadius of Agen wrote Contra Arianos in 357-358 against the second creed of Sirmium (the 'blasphemy' of 357): the Gallic Nicene position argued before Ariminum by a bishop who then sat at Ariminum (359), was deceived into signing, and recanted; the editor's notice at file lines 15505-15546 rehearses this from Jerome and Sulpicius. De fide orthodoxa and the second De fide printed with it are of disputed authorship. The Caesarius and Didymus texts are outside the Shared-Events brief and were not checked. A primary Gallic voice from before the council, not a narrative of it.

redundaret.

passus  est,  quem  Filius  Dei  suscepit,  quem  induit,  quem

minis,  quod  caducum.

homo  potius  in  Deum.
